DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Description of organization

Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay ("Manor") is organized as a wholly-owned, nonprofit
subsidiary of The Saint Elizabeth Community ("Community"), a nonprofit organization.
Manor's operations consist principally of owning and operating a 133-bed nursing
facility located in Bristol, Rhode Island,

Recently issued but not yet effective accounting pronouncements

Revenue recognition

In May 2014, the Financial Accounting Standards Board ("FASB") issued ASU No.
2014-09, Revenue from Contracts with Customers (“ASU 2014-09"), which requires an entity
to recognize the amount of revenue to which it expects to be entitled for the transfer of
promised goods or services to customers. This ASU will replace most existing revenue
recognition guidance in accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of
America ("U.S. GAAP"), including industry-specific guidance, when it becomes
effective. This new guidance is effective for years beginning after December 15, 2018.
Manor is evaluating the effect that ASU 2014-09 will have on its financial statements
and related disclosures.

Financial statement presentation

In August 2016, the FASB issued ASU No. 2016-14, Presentation of Financial Statements of
Not-For Profit Entities (“ASU 2016-14”), which amends the standards for the
presentation and accompanying disclosures of financial statements of nonprofit
organizations. The ASU is effective for years beginning after December 15, 2017, and
will be applied on a retrospective basis. After the adoption of ASU 2016-14, Manor’s
financial statements will report two classes of net assets, distinguishing between net
assets with donor restrictions and those without donor restrictions. The financial
statements will also include an analysis of expenses by function and nature, and provide
enhanced disclosures about Manor’s liquidity and availability of its resources, and donor
restrictions and designations of its net assets.

Use of estimates

The preparation of financial statements in conformity with U.S. GAAP requires
management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of
assets and liabilities, and disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of the
financial statements. Estimates also affect the reported amounts of revenue and
expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could differ from those estimates.

Net assets

Manor's net assets and activities that increase or decrease net assets are classified as
either unrestricted, temporarily restricted or permanently restricted:

Unrestricted net assets are those without any donor-imposed restrictions as to their
use and are available for the general operations of Manor.

===== PAGE 339 =====
[OCR]
NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
DECEMBER 31, 2017

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

Net assets (continued)

Temporarily restricted net assets are those whose use by Manor has been limited by
donors to a specific time petiod or purpose. At December 31, 2017, Manor had
$71,000 of temporarily restricted net assets restricted for the purchase of equipment.

Permanently restricted net assets have been restricted by donots to be maintained by

Manor in perpetuity. At December 31, 2017, Manor had no permanently restricted
net assets.

Revenue, gains and other support are reported as increases in unrestricted net assets
unless use of the related assets is limited by donor-imposed restrictions or by law.
Expenses are reported as decreases in unrestricted net assets. Gains and losses on
investments or other assets or liabilities are reported as increases or decreases in
unrestricted net assets unless their use is restricted by explicit donor stipulation or by
law.

Operating loss

The statement of operations and changes in net asset deficiency includes "operating
loss." Changes in unrestricted net asset deficiency which are excluded from "operating
loss," consistent with industry practice, include transfers to and from Community.

Contributions and net assets released from restrictions

Contribution revenue is recorded as received or unconditionally promised. If the
contribution is made in assets other than cash, the amount of the contribution is
measured at the fair value of the asset contributed at the date the contribution or
unconditional promise to give is made by the donor.

Contributions of cash, including collections of any future unconditional promises to
give, and other assets are reported as restricted support if they ate received with donor
stipulations that limit the use of the donated assets. When a donor restriction expires,
that is, when a stipulated time restriction ends or purpose restriction is accomplished,
temporarily restricted net assets are reclassified to unrestricted net assets and reported in
the statement of operations and changes in net asset deficiency as "net assets released
from restrictions." Donor-restricted contributions whose restrictions are met in the
same reporting period are reported as unrestricted support.

Gifts of long-lived assets such as land, buildings, or equipment are reported as
unrestricted support unless explicit donor stipulations specify how the donated assets
must be used. Gifts of long-lived assets with explicit restrictions that specify how the
assets are to be used and gifts of cash or other assets that must be used to acquire long-
lived assets are reported as restricted support. Absent explicit donor stipulations about
how long those long-lived assets must be maintained, expirations of donor restrictions
are reported when the donated or acquired long-lived assets are placed in service.

[OCR]
NOTE 1.

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT

Cash and cash equivalents, and concentration of credit risk

Manor considers all highly liquid investments with original maturities of three months
ot less to be cash equivalents. Manor had no cash equivalents at December 31, 2017.

At December 31, 2017, Manor had approximately $518,000 on deposit in excess of
amounts insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation.

Net patient service revenue

Net patient service revenue is reported at estimated net amounts realizable from
residents, third-party payors (principally Medicare and Rhode Island Medicaid) and
others for services rendered.

Manor’s third-party revenue is paid principally at prospectively determined rates.
Retroactive adjustments are considered in the recognition of revenue on an estimated
basis in the period the related services ate rendered; any differences between the
estimated amounts accrued and interim and final settlements are adjusted in future
periods as adjustments become known ot as yeats are no longer subject to such audits,
reviews and investigations.

Laws and regulations governing Medicare, Medicaid and other governmental
reimbursement programs are extremely complex and subject to interpretation. As a
result, there is at least a reasonable possibility that recorded estimates could change by a
material amount in the near term. It is management’s opinion that Manor is in
compliance with applicable laws and regulations; however, the possibility of government
review and interpretation exists. Compliance with such laws and regulations can be
subject to future government review and interpretation, as well as significant regulatory
action; failure to comply with such laws and regulations can result in fines, penalties and
exclusion from Medicare and Medicaid programs.

Net patient service revenue from third-party payors totaled $10,440,408 for the year
ended December 31, 2017.

Third-party advances

Manor records advances from third-party payors as a liability upon receipt.

===== PAGE 341 =====
[OCR]
NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
DECEMBER 31, 2017

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

Patient services accounts receivable
Patient services accounts receivable are stated at the amount Manor expects to collect.

Under Rhode Island General Law Title 40, Chapter 8, Section 6.1(b)(2), Human Services,
Medical Assistance, Provider Care During Pendency of Application, Uncompensated Care While
Determination is Overdue, upon Manor’s request, the State of Rhode Island (the "State"') is
required to make payments to Manor for charges related to a patient that Manor has
determined should be eligible for Medicaid and for whom a completed application for
Medicaid benefits filed with the State has been pending for 90 days or more ("advance
payments"); such advance payments may not be recovered, offset or recouped by the
State upon a subsequent determination by the State that the patient is not eligible for
Medicaid. Consequently, advance payments received by Manor prior to the date the
State has made a determination related to the patient’s eligibility for Medicaid are offset
against the patient services receivables included in the accompanying balance sheet.
Advance payments teceived by Manor related to Medicaid claims subsequently
approved and paid by the State are required to be repaid to the State through an
upcoming reconciliation process and, consequently, are reported within current
liabilities, third-party advances in the accompanying balance sheet. At December 31,
2017, advance payments offset against patient services receivables totaled $488,369.

Manor provides for probable uncollectible amounts through a charge to bad debt
expense and a credit to the allowance for doubtful accounts based on its assessment of
the current status of individual accounts. Balances that are still outstanding after Manor
has used reasonable collection efforts are written off through a charge to the allowance
for doubtful accounts and a credit to accounts receivable. Manor does not obtain
collateral for amounts due from providing patient services.

At December 31, 2017, approximately 16% of Manor's accounts receivable is due from
the Rhode Island Medicaid program.

Assets limited as to use

Assets limited as to use are held by trustees, principally in money market accounts,
under trust agreements in connection with a self-insurance arrangement consisting of
funds held by Citizens Bank and The Washington Trust Company in the name of Saint
Elizabeth Community Workers' Compensation Trust.

Property and equipment

Property and equipment are stated at cost, less accumulated depreciation. Depreciation
is provided using the straight-line method over the estimated useful lives of the assets,

ranging from 4 years to 25 years. Depreciation expense totaled $603,390 for the year
ended December 31, 2017.

Property and equipment (continued

Manor evaluates long-lived assets held and used by Manor for impairment whenever
events or changes in circumstances indicate that the carrying amount of the asset may
not be recoverable. An impaitment loss is recognized if the sum of the expected
undiscounted future cash flows from the use and disposition of the asset is less than its
carrying amount. Generally, the amount of impairment loss is measured as the
difference between the carrying amount of the asset and the estimated fair value of the
asset. Manor did not record an impairment loss during the year ended December 31,
2017,

Estimated workers' compensation self-insurance costs

The liability for workers' compensation self-insurance costs includes estimates of the
ultimate costs for both reported claims and claims incurred but not reported.

It is Manor's policy to fund workers' compensation insurance costs accrued.

Defined contribution plan

Community sponsors a defined contribution 403(6) plan (see Note 5) covering
employees meeting certain age and length of service requirements. It is Manor's policy
to fund pension costs accrued. Contributions to this plan are determined at the
discretion of the Board of Trustees.

Debt issuance costs

Debt issuance costs, net of accumulated amortization, are reported as a direct reduction
of the debt obligation to which such costs relate. Amortization of debt issuance costs is
reported as a component of interest expense and is computed on the straight-line
method, which approximates the effective interest method.

Income taxes

Manor qualifies as a tax-exempt organization under Section 501(c)(3) of the Internal
Revenue Code. As a not-for-profit entity, Manor is subject to unrelated business
income tax ("UBIT"), if applicable. In accordance with FASB Accounting Standards
Codification ("ASC") 740, Income Taxes, Manor applies the “more likely than not”
threshold to the recognition and derecognition of tax positions for its financial
statements. Management has evaluated Manot’s tax positions and has concluded that
there were no uncertain tax positions that qualified for either recognition or disclosure
in these financial statements.

Manor files tax returns in the U.S. federal jurisdiction.

Subsequent events

Manor has evaluated subsequent events through May 8, 2018, the date that the
accompanying financial statements were available to be issued. Except as disclosed in
Note 3, there were no material subsequent events that required recognition or disclosure
in these financial statements.

RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS
The Saint Elizabeth Community

Community is organized exclusively for the charitable purpose of supporting, benefiting
and making distributions from its assets to its subsidiaries, which consist of Manor;
Saint Elizabeth Home, East Greenwich ("Home") (which owns and operates a 120-bed
nursing facility located in East Greenwich, Rhode Island); Saint Elizabeth Development
Corporation ("Development") (which is a subsidiary of Home and which owns land in
East Greenwich, Rhode Island where Home operates its nursing facility); Saint
Elizabeth Place, Providence ("Place") (which owns and operates a 149-unit apartment
complex for the elderly located in Providence, Rhode Island); St. Elizabeth Assisted
Living, L.P. d/b/a Saint Elizabeth Court ("Court") (which owns and operates a 69-unit
low and moderate-income assisted living facility located in Providence, Rhode Island);
Cornerstone Adult Services, Inc. (which provides adult day care services in several
Rhode Island locations); Saint Elizabeth Terrace ("Terrace") (which owns a 34-unit
elderly housing apartment complex in Warwick, Rhode Island); and Saint Elizabeth at
Home Corp. (which operates a licensed home health nursing care provider).
Community is also the sole shareholder of SEAL Melrose, Inc. ("SEAL") (which is the
general partner of Court). All subsidiaries, except for SEAL and Court, are organized as
nonprofit organizations.

During the year ended December 31, 2017, Community's net assets increased by

Shared services

Home provides Manor with administrative and development services; Manor
reimbutses Home at an amount equal to Home's cost. The cost, including payroll taxes
and employee benefits, totaled $521,924 for the year ended December 31, 2017, and is
included principally in payroll and contracted services in the accompanying statement of
operations and changes in net asset deficiency.

Self-insurance

Community maintains a pooled self-insurance plan for workers' compensation costs.
The plan requires the payment of certain administration, claims and risk management
costs, as well as payment for losses. Manor and Home are the principal members of the
plan and make monthly contributions (premiums) to pay for the costs and bond fees, as
well as to establish a cumulative reserve for future claims. The reserve is held in trust
and invested by the plan trustee. Upon termination, any monies in excess of amounts
needed to pay claims and expenses will be returned to the members. The trustee
maintains separate accountability for Manor's allocable portion of contributions, costs,
losses and cumulative reserve for future claims. Manor's cost under the plan totaled
$215,950 for the year ended December 31, 2017.

MEDICAID ELIGIBILITY DETERMINATION DELAYS AND
CONTINGENCY

Medicaid eligibility determination delays

Since early 2016, the State has been significantly delayed in rendering Medicaid eligibility
determinations with respect to patients that Manor has determined should be eligible for
Medicaid and for whom a completed application for Medicaid benefits has been filed
with the State. Manor provides services to these patients and recognizes related revenue
during the period in which the State has not made a determination related to a
completed application filed with the State (“Medicaid Pending Application”). Amounts
reported as patient services receivables related to Medicaid Pending Applications that
have been pending for less than 90 days and those that have been pending for 90 days
or mote for which Manor has not requested an advance payment (see Note 1) may be
subject to subsequent denial by the State at some future date. Management believes that
the State will ultimately determine that patients for whom a Medicaid Pending
Application exists are eligible for Medicaid and that any potential loss related to
Medicaid Pending Applications subsequently denied by the State will not have a material
adverse effect on Manor’s financial position.

Continuing operations

The accompanying financial statements have been prepared assuming that Manor will
continue as a going concern. Manor has incurred multiple consecutive years of losses
which have resulted in a net asset deficiency. The occupancy rate is improving and
Community has the willingness and financial ability to provide further support to Manor
as needed to fund Manor's continuing operations. Therefore, doubt about Manor's
ability to continue as a going concern has been alleviated.

INDEPENDENT AUDITOR'S REPORT

To the Board of Trustees
The Saint Elizabeth Community
East Greenwich, Rhode Island

We have audited the accompanying financial statements of Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay ("Manor"),
which comprise the balance sheet as of December 31, 2018, and the related statements of operations,

functional expenses, and cash flows for the year then ended, and the related notes to the financial
statements.

Management's Responsibility for the Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these financial statements in
accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America; this includes
the design, implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the preparation and fair
presentation of financial statements that are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or
error,

Auditor's Responsibility

Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these financial statements based on our audit. We
conducted our audit in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United States of
America. Those standatds require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance
about whether the financial statements are free from material misstatement.

An audit involves performing procedures to obtain audit evidence about the amounts and disclosures in
the financial statements. The procedures selected depend on the auditor's judgment, including the
assessment of the risks of material misstatement of the financial statements, whether due to fraud or
etrot. In making those risk assessments, the auditor considers internal control relevant to the entity's
preparation and fair presentation of the financial statements in order to design audit procedures that are
appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness
of the entity's internal control. Accordingly, we express no such opinion. An audit also includes
evaluating the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of significant

accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall presentation of the
financial statements.

We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for
our audit opinion.

NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
DECEMBER 31, 2018

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Description of organization

Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay ("Manor") is organized as a wholly-owned, nonprofit
subsidiary of The Saint Elizabeth Community ("Community"), a nonprofit organization.
Manor's operations consist principally of owning and operating a 133-bed nursing
facility located in Bristol, Rhode Island.

Recently adopted accounting pronouncement

On August 18, 2016, the Financial Accounting Standards Board ("FASB") issued
Accounting Standards Update ("ASU") No. 2016-14, Not-for-Profit Entities (Topic 958)
— Presentation of Financial Statements of Not-for-Profit Entities. The update addresses the
complexity and understandability of net asset classification, deficiencies in information
about liquidity and availability of resources, and the lack of consistency in the type of
information provided about expenses and investment return. Manor has adjusted the
presentation of these financial statements accordingly.

Recently issued but not yet effective accounting pronouncement

In May 2014, the FASB issued ASU No. 2014-09, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
(Topic 606), as amended (commonly referred to as "ASC 606"), which requires an entity
to recognize revenue to depict the transfer of promised goods or services to customers
in an amount that reflects the consideration which the entity expects to be entitled to
receive in exchange for those goods or services. ASC 606 replaces most existing
revenue tecognition standards in U.S. GAAP, including industry-specific standards,
when it becomes effective. For annual reporting periods, this standard is effective for
Manot on January 1, 2019. The standard permits the use of either the retrospective or
cumulative-effect transition method. Manor is currently completing its initial assessment
and evaluation of the impact that ASC 606 will have on Manor’s financial statements
and related disclosures.

The Manor expects to retrospectively adopt ASC 606 effective January 1, 2019. Asa
result, substantially all amounts previously reported as provision for losses on patient
services teceivables will now be considered an implicit price concession and will be
recognized as a reduction of net patient service revenue. The adoption of ASC 606 will
also result in expanded disclosures that will enable the users of the financial statements
to better understand the nature, amount, timing and uncertainty, if any, of revenues and
cash flows arising from contracts with customers.

Use of estimates

The preparation of financial statements in conformity with U.S. GAAP requires
management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of
assets and liabilities, and disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of the
financial statements. Estimates also affect the reported amounts of revenue and
expenses during the reporting period. Actual results could differ from those estimates.

===== PAGE 357 =====
[OCR]
NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
DECEMBER 31, 2018

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

Basis of presentation

The financial statements of Manor have been prepared in accordance with U.S. GAAP,
which require Manor to report information regatding its financial position and activities
according to the following net asset classifications:

Net Assets Without Donor Restrictions - Net assets available for use in general operations
and not subject to donor (or certain grantor) restrictions.

Net Assets With Donor Restrictions - Net assets subject to donor-imposed restrictions.
Some donor-imposed restrictions are temporary in nature, such as those that will be met
by the passage of time or other events specified by the donor. Other donor-imposed
restrictions ate perpetual in nature, where the donor stipulates that resources be
maintained in perpetuity. Gifts of long-lived assets and gifts of cash restricted for the
acquisition of long-lived assets are recognized as revenue when the assets are placed in
service.

Revenue, gains and other support are reported as increases in net assets without donor
restrictions unless use of the related assets is limited by donor-imposed restrictions or
by law. Expenses are reported as decreases in net assets without donot restrictions.

Manor reports contributions restricted by donors as increases in net assets without
donor restrictions if the restrictions expire (that is, when a stipulated time restriction
ends or purpose testriction is accomplished) in the reporting period in which the
revenue is recognized.

Operating loss

The statement of operations includes "operating loss." Changes in net asset deficiency
which are excluded from "operating loss," consistent with industry practice, include
transfers to and from Community.

Contributions

Contribution revenue is recorded as received or unconditionally promised. If the
contribution is made in assets other than cash, the amount of the contribution is
measured at the fair value of the asset contributed at the date the contribution or
unconditional promise to give is made by the donor.

Contributions of cash, including collections of any future unconditional promises to
give, and other assets are reported as restricted support if they are received with donor
stipulations that limit the use of the donated assets. When a donor restriction expires,
that is, when a stipulated time restriction ends or purpose restriction is accomplished,
net assets with donor restrictions are reclassified to net assets without donor restrictions
and reported in the statement of operations as net assets released from restrictions.

NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

Cash and cash equivalents, and concentration of credit risk

Manor considers all highly liquid investments with original maturities of three months
or less to be cash equivalents. Manor had no cash equivalents at December 31, 2018.

At December 31, 2018, Manor had approximately $415,000 on deposit in excess of
amounts insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation.

Net patient service revenue

Net patient service revenue is reported at estimated net amounts realizable from
residents, third-party payors (principally Medicare and Rhode Island Medicaid) and
others for services rendered.

Manor’s third-party revenue is paid principally at prospectively determined tates.
Retroactive adjustments are considered in the recognition of revenue on an estimated
basis in the period the related services are rendered; any differences between the
estimated amounts accrued and interim and final settlements are adjusted in future
periods as adjustments become known or as years ate no longer subject to such audits,
reviews and investigations.

Laws and regulations governing Medicare, Medicaid and other governmental
reimbursement programs are extremely complex and subject to interpretation. As a
result, there is at least a reasonable possibility that recorded estimates could change by a
material amount in the near term. It is management’s opinion that Manor is in
compliance with applicable laws and regulations; however, the possibility of
government review and interpretation exists. Compliance with such laws and
regulations can be subject to future government review and interpretation, as well as
significant regulatory action; failure to comply with such laws and regulations can result
in fines, penalties and exclusion from Medicare and Medicaid programs.

Net patient service revenue from third-party payors totaled $9,830,270 for the year
ended December 31, 2018.

Third-party advances

Manor records advances from third-party payors as a liability upon receipt.

Estimated amounts due to third party payors

Estimated amounts due to third-party payors are principally related to third-party
payments in excess of outstanding balances which are finalized annually after year-end
concludes, and are typically received or funded by the end of the following calendar
year.

===== PAGE 359 =====
[OCR]
NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
DECEMBER 31, 2018

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

Patient services accounts receivable
Patient services accounts receivable are stated at the amount Manor expects to collect.

Under Rhode Island General Law Title 40, Chapter 8, Section 6.1(b)(2), Human Services,
Medical Assistance, Provider Care During Pendency of Application, Uncompensated Care While
Determination is Overdue, upon Manor’s request, the State of Rhode Island (the "State") is
required to make payments to Manor for charges related to a patient that Manor has
determined should be eligible for Medicaid and for whom a completed application for
Medicaid benefits filed with the State has been pending for 90 days or more ("advance
payments"); such advance payments may not be recovered, offset or recouped by the
State upon a subsequent determination by the State that the patient is not eligible for
Medicaid. Consequently, advance payments received by Manor prior to the date the
State has made a determination related to the patient’s eligibility for Medicaid are offset
against the patient services receivables included in the accompanying balance sheet.
Advance payments received by Manor related to Medicaid claims subsequently
approved and paid by the State are required to be repaid to the State through an
ongoing reconciliation process and, consequently, ate reported within current liabilities,
third-party advances in the accompanying balance sheet. At December 31, 2018,
advance payments offset against patient services receivables totaled $426,918.

Manor provides for probable uncollectible amounts through a charge to bad debt
expense and a credit to the allowance for doubtful accounts based on its assessment of
the current status of individual accounts. Balances that are still outstanding after Manor
has used reasonable collection efforts are written off through a charge to the allowance
for doubtful accounts and a credit to accounts receivable. Manor does not obtain
collateral for amounts due from providing patient services.

At December 31, 2018, approximately 38% of Manor's accounts receivable is due from
the Rhode Island Medicaid program.

Assets limited as to use

Assets limited as to use are held by trustees, principally in money market accounts,
under trust agreements in connection with a self-insurance arrangement consisting of
funds held by Citizens Bank and The Washington Trust Company in the name of Saint
Elizabeth Community Workers' Compensation Trust.

Property and equipment

Property and equipment are stated at cost, less accumulated depreciation. Depreciation
is provided using the straight-line method over the estimated useful lives of the assets,
ranging from 4 years to 25 years. Depreciation expense totaled $615,840 for the year
ended December 31, 2018.

Property and equipment (continued)

Manor evaluates long-lived assets held and used by Manor for impairment whenever
events or changes in circumstances indicate that the carrying amount of the asset may
not be recoverable. An impairment loss is recognized if the sum of the expected
undiscounted future cash flows from the use and disposition of the asset is less than its
carrying amount. Generally, the amount of impairment loss is measured as the
difference between the carrying amount of the asset and the estimated fair value of the

asset. Manor did not record an impairment loss during the year ended December 31,
2018.

Estimated workers' compensation self-insurance costs

The liability for workers' compensation self-insurance costs includes estimates of the
ultimate costs for both reported claims and claims incurred but not reported.

It is Manor's policy to fund workers' compensation insurance costs accrued.

Debt issuance costs

Debt issuance costs, net of accumulated amortization, are reported as a direct reduction
of the debt obligation to which such costs relate. Amortization of debt issuance costs is
reported as a component of interest expense and is computed on the straight-line
method, which approximates the effective interest method.

Functional allocation of expenses

The costs of program and supporting services activities have been summarized on a
functional basis in the statement of operations. The statement of functional expenses
presents the natural classification detail of expenses by function. Accordingly, certain
costs have been allocated among the programs and supporting services benefited.

The expenses that are allocated include the following:

Expense Method of Allocation
Payroll Time and effort
Payroll taxes and employee benefits Time and effort
Insurance Time and effort

Income taxes

Manor qualifies as a tax-exempt organization under Section 501(c)(3) of the Internal
Revenue Code. As a not-for-profit entity, Manor is subject to unrelated business
income tax ("UBIT"), if applicable. In accordance with FASB Accounting Standards
Codification ("ASC") 740, Income Taxes, Manor applies the "more likely than not"
threshold to the recognition and derecognition of tax positions for its financial
statements. Management has evaluated Manor’s tax positions and has concluded that
there were no uncertain tax positions that qualified for either recognition or disclosure
in these financial statements.

Manor files tax returns in the U.S. federal jurisdiction.

Subsequent events

Manor has evaluated subsequent events through August 8, 2019, the date that the
accompanying financial statements were available to be issued. There were no material
subsequent events that required recognition or disclosure in these financial statements.

RELATED-PARTY TRANSACTIONS
The Saint Elizabeth Community

Community is organized exclusively for the charitable purpose of supporting, benefiting
and making distributions from its assets to its subsidiaries, which consist of Manor;
Saint Elizabeth Home, East Greenwich ("Home") (which owns and operates a 168-bed
nutsing facility located in East Greenwich, Rhode Island); Saint Elizabeth Development
Corporation ("Development") (which is a subsidiary of Home and which owns land in
East Greenwich, Rhode Island where Home operates its nursing facility); Saint
Elizabeth Place, Providence ("Place") (which owns and operates a 149-unit apartment
complex for the elderly located in Providence, Rhode Island); St. Elizabeth Assisted
Living, L-P. d/b/a Saint Elizabeth Court ("Court") (which owns and operates a 69-unit
low and moderate-income assisted living facility located in Providence, Rhode Island);
Cornerstone Adult Services, Inc. (which provides adult day care services in several
Rhode Island locations); Saint Elizabeth Terrace ('Tertace") (which owns a 34-unit
elderly housing apartment complex in Warwick, Rhode Island); and Saint Elizabeth at
Home Corp. (which operates a licensed home health nursing care provider).
Community is also the sole shareholder of SEAL Melrose, Inc. ("SEAL") (which is the
general partner of Court). All subsidiaries, except for SEAL and Court, are organized as
nonprofit organizations.

In a ptior year, Community provided Manor approximately $558,000 non-interest
bearing cash for operating needs and an extended timeframe for repayment. There are
no tepayment terms.

RELATED-PARTY TRANSACTIONS (CONTINUED)
The Saint Elizabeth Community (continued)

During the year ended December 31, 2018, Community's net assets decreased by
$3,475,058. During the year ended December 31, 2018, Community provided support
to Manor of $1,042,038 for operations and $142,100 for property and equipment.

Shared services

Home provides Manor with administrative and development services; Manor
reimburses Home at an amount equal to Home's cost. The cost, including payroll taxes
and employee benefits, totaled $614,690 for the year ended December 31, 2018, and is
included principally in payroll and contracted services in the accompanying statement of
operations.

Self-insurance

Community maintains a pooled self-insurance plan for workers’ compensation costs.
The plan requires the payment of certain administration, claims and risk management
costs, as well as payment for losses. Manor and Home are the principal members of the
plan and make monthly contributions (premiums) to pay for the costs and bond fees, as
well as to establish a cumulative reserve for future claims. The reserve is held in trust
and invested by the plan trustee. Upon termination, any monies in excess of amounts
needed to pay claims and expenses will be returned to the membets. The trustee
maintains sepatate accountability for Manor's allocable portion of contributions, costs,
losses and cumulative reserve for future claims. Manor's cost under the plan totaled
$215,808 for the year ended December 31, 2018.

CareLink, Inc.

Manor contracted for patient services totaling $776,061 from CareLink, Inc., a
nonstock, nonprofit organization in which Community has a nonconttolling
investment. Such expenses are included in contracted services in the accompanying
statement of operations.

PENSION PLANS

Community sponsors a defined contribution 403(b) plan in which Manor participates.
Included in payroll taxes and employee benefits in the accompanying statement of
functional expenses is pension expense of $146,313 for the defined contribution 403(b)
plan.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY

MEDICAID ELIGIBILITY DETERMINATION DELAYS AND
CONTINGENCY

Medicaid eligibility determination delays

Since early 2016, the State has been significantly delayed in rendering Medicaid eligibility
determinations with respect to patients that Manor has determined should be eligible
for Medicaid and for whom a completed application for Medicaid benefits has been
filed with the State. Manor provides services to these patients and recognizes related
revenue during the period in which the State has not made a determination related to a
completed application filed with the State ("Medicaid Pending Application"). Amounts
reported as patient services receivables related to Medicaid Pending Applications that
have been pending for less than 90 days and those that have been pending for 90 days
or more for which Manor has not requested an advance payment (see Note 1) may be
subject to subsequent denial by the State at some future date. Management believes
that the State will ultimately determine that patients for whom a Medicaid Pending
Application exists are cligible for Medicaid and that any potential loss related to
Medicaid Pending Applications subsequently denied by the State will not have a material
adverse effect on Manot’s financial position.

Continuing operations

The accompanying financial statements have been prepared assuming that Manor will
continue as a going concern. Manor has incurred multiple consecutive years of losses
which have resulted in a net asset deficiency. The occupancy rate is improving, the
maturing note payable, bank is being refinanced with the bank, and Community has the
willingness and financial ability to provide further support to Manor as needed to fund
Manor's continuing operations and cash flow needs. Normal operations are dependent
on the successful implementation of the aforementioned factors.

INDEPENDENT AUDITOR'S REPORT

To the Boatd of Trustees
The Saint Elizabeth Community
East Greenwich, Rhode Island

We have audited the accompanying financial statements of Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay ("Manot"),
which comprise the balance sheet as of December 31, 2019, and the related statements of operations,

functional expenses, and cash flows for the year then ended, and the related notes to the financial
statements.

Management's Responsibility for the Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these financial statements in
accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America; this includes
the design, implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the preparation and fair

presentation of financial statements that are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or
etror.

Auditor's Responsibility

Our tesponsibility is to express an opinion on these financial statements based on out audit. We
conducted our audit in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United States of
America. Those standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance
about whether the financial statements are free from material misstatement.

An audit involves petforming procedures to obtain audit evidence about the amounts and disclosures in
the financial statements. The procedures selected depend on the auditor's judgment, including the
assessment of the risks of material misstatement of the financial statements, whether due to fraud or
error. In making those risk assessments, the auditor considers internal control relevant to the entity's
pfeparation and fair presentation of the financial statements in order to design audit procedures that are
appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness
of the entity's internal control. Accordingly, we express no such opinion. An audit also includes
evaluating the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of significant
accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall presentation of the
financial statements.

We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for
out audit opinion.

CITRIN COOPERMAN & COMPARY, LLP

Opinion
In our opinion, the financial statements referred to above present fairly, in all material respects, the
financial position of Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay as of December 31, 2019, and the changes in its

net assets and its cash flows for the year then ended, in accordance with accounting principles generally
accepted in the United States of America.

Adoption of New Accounting Pronouncements

As discussed in Note 2 to the financial statements, Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay adopted
Accounting Standards Codification Topic 606, Revenue from Contracts with Customers and the amendments
in ASU No. 2018-08, Clarifying the Scope of Accounting Guidance for Contributions Received and Contributions
Made, as of January 1, 2019. Our opinion is not modified with respect to these matters.

Subsequent Event

As discussed in Note 8 to the financial statement, subsequent to yeat-end, the World Health
Organization has declared COVID-19 to constitute a "Public Health Emergency of International
Concern." Given the uncertainty of the situation, the duration of any business disruption and related
financial impact cannot be reasonably estimated at this time. Our opinion is not modified with respect
to this matter.

Providence, Rhode Island
October 2, 2020

NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
DECEMBER 31, 2019

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Description of organization

Saint Elizabeth Manor, East Bay ("Manor") is organized as a wholly-owned, nonprofit
subsidiary of The Saint Elizabeth Community ("Community"), a nonprofit organization.
Manor's operations consist principally of owning and operating a 133-bed nursing
facility located in Bristol, Rhode Island.

Recently adopted accounting pronouncements

Revenue recognition

In May 2014, the Financial Accounting Standards Board ("FASB") issued Accounting
Standards Update (“ASU”) No. 2014-09, Revenue from Contracts with Customers (“Topic
606”), with several clarifying updates issued subsequently. In conjunction with Topic
606, a new subtopic, Accounting Standards Codification ("ASC") 340-40, Other Assets
and Deferred Costs — Contracts mith Customers, was also issued. The updated standard
replaces most existing revenue recognition and certain cost guidance under accounting
principles generally accepted in the United States of America ("U.S. GAAP").
Collectively, we refer to Topic 606 and Subtopic 340-40 as “ASC 606”. ASC 606
amends existing accounting standards for revenue recognition and establishes principles
for recognizing revenue upon the transfer of promised goods or services to customers
based on the expected consideration to be received in exchange for those goods and
services. Manor adopted ASC 606 effective January 1, 2019 using the modified
retrospective transition method. Manor has determined that the adjustments using the
modified retrospective approach did not have a material impact on the date of the initial
application. Manor did not apply any practical expedients in implementing ASC 606.

Contributions

In June 2018, FASB issued ASU 2018-08, Clarifying the Scope and Accounting Guidance for
Contributions Received and Contributions Made ("ASU 2018-08"), which provides guidance
for determining whether a transaction should be accounted for as a contribution or an
exchange transaction, and whether a contribution is conditional or unconditional. This
ASU is effective for years beginning after December 15, 2018. Manor adopted ASU
2018-08 effective January 1, 2019 using the modified prospective method. Under the
modified prospective method, the amendments are applied to agreements that are either
not completed as of the effective date or entered into after the effective date. Manor
has determined that the application of the amendments of ASU 2018-08 did not have a
material impact on Manor's financial statements and related disclosures.

Use of estimates

The preparation of financial statements in conformity with U.S. GAAP requires
management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of
assets and liabilities, and disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities at the date of the
financial statements. [Estimates also affect the reported amounts of revenue and
expenses during the reporting petiod. Actual results could differ from those estimates.

===== PAGE 375 =====
[OCR]
NOTE 1.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY

DECEMBER 31, 2019

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT
ACCOUNTING POLICIES (CONTINUED)

Basis of presentation

The financial statements of Manor have been prepared in accordance with U.S. GAAP,
which require Manor to report information regarding its financial position and activities
according to the following net asset classifications:

Net Assets Without Donor Restrictions - Net assets available for use in general operations
and not subject to donor (or certain grantor) restrictions.

Net Assets With Donor Restrictions - Net assets subject to donor-imposed restrictions.
Some donor-imposed restrictions are temporary in nature, such as those that will be met
by the passage of time or other events specified by the donor. Other donor-imposed
restrictions are perpetual in nature, where the donor stipulates that resources be
maintained in perpetuity. Gifts of long-lived assets and gifts of cash restricted for the
acquisition of long-lived assets ate recognized as revenue when the assets are placed in
service. At December 31, 2019, there were no net assets with donor restrictions.

Revenue is reported as increases in net assets without donor restrictions unless use of
the related assets is limited by donor-imposed restrictions. Expenses are reported as
decreases in net assets without donor restrictions. Gains and losses on assets or
liabilities are reported as increases or decreases in net assets without donor restrictions
unless their use is restricted by explicit donor stipulations or by law. Expirations of net
assets with donor restrictions (i.¢., the donor-stipulated purpose has been fulfilled
and/or the stipulated time period has elapsed) are reported as net assets released from
donor restrictions.

Operating loss

The statement of operations includes "operating loss." Changes in net asset deficiency
which are excluded from "operating loss," consistent with industry practice, include
transfers to and from Community.

Contributions

Contributions of cash and other assets ate presented as restricted support if they are
received with donor stipulations that limit the use of the donated assets. When a donor
restriction expires, that is, when a stipulated time restriction ends or purpose restriction
is accomplished, net assets with donor restrictions are reclassified to net assets without
donot restrictions and reported in the statement of operations as net assets released
from restrictions.

Contributions, including unconditional promises to give, ate recognized as revenues in
the period received. Conditional promises to give, that is, those with a measurable
performance or other barrier, are not recognized until they become unconditional, that
is, at the time when the conditions on which they depend are substantially met.

Grants and contributions received with donor-imposed or grantor-imposed restrictions
that are fulfilled in the same year as received are reported as support without donor
restrictions.

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT

Cash and cash equivalents, and concentration of credit risk

Manor considers all highly liquid investments with original maturities of three months
ot less to be cash equivalents. Manor had no cash equivalents at December 31, 2019.

At December 31, 2019, Manor had approximately $362,000 on deposit in excess of
amounts insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation.

Revenue recognition

Saint Elizabeth Manor adopted ASC Topic 606, Revenue from Contracts with Customers, on
January 1, 2019, Revenue is measured based on consideration specified in a contract
with a customer, and excludes any amounts collected on behalf of third parties. Manor
recognizes revenue when it satisfies a performance obligation by transferring control
over a product or service to a customer. The majority of Manor’s revenue originates
from contracts with customers to provide various healthcare services. The majority of
Manot’s services represent a bundle of services that are not capable of being distinct
and as such, are treated as a single performance obligation that is satisfied over time as

the services are rendered. Manor’s standard payment tetms are within 30 days from
billing,

Manor determines the transaction price based on contractually agreed upon rates,
adjusted for estimates of variable consideration, such as implicit price concessions.
Variable consideration also exists as Manot’s third-party revenue is paid principally at
prospectively determined rates. Retroactive adjustments are considered in the
recognition of revenue on an estimated basis in the period the related services are
rendered such that net patient service revenues are only recorded to the extent that it is
probable that a significant reversal in the amount of cumulative revenue recognized will
not occur in the future. Any differences between the estimated amounts accrued, and
interim and final settlements are adjusted in future periods as adjustments become
known or as years are no longer subject to such audits, reviews and investigations.

Disaggregation of revenues

Manor operates as a skilled nursing facility and provides services to patients, principally
from Rhode Island, who have third-party financial coverage (principally Rhode Island
Medicaid and Medicare), as well as to other patients who are responsible for their own
financial obligations. Therefore, Manor’s viability is dependent on a number of factors
such as licensed bed capacity, occupancy rates, mix of patient by payer and case type,
the rates of reimbursement, changes to Medicaid and Medicare programs, and Manot’s
ability to collect on its contracts. All of Manor’s net patient service revenue for financial
reporting purposes are recorded over time as the services are rendered.

Net patient service revenue from third-party payors totaled $10,127,330 for the year
ended December 31, 2019. Net patient service revenue from ptivate payors totaled
$4,590,683 for the year ended December 31, 2019.

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT

Significant risks and uncertainties

Laws and regulations governing Medicare, Medicaid and other governmental
reimbursement programs are extremely complex and subject to interpretation. As a
result, there is at least a reasonable possibility that recorded estimates could change by a
material amount in the near term. It is management’s opinion that Saint Elizabeth
Manor is in compliance with applicable laws and regulations; however, the possibility of
government review and interpretation exists. Compliance with such laws and regulations
can be subject to future government review and interpretation, as well as significant
regulatory action; failure to comply with such laws and regulations can result in fines,
penalties and exclusion from Medicare and Medicaid programs.

Estimated amounts due to third-party payors

Estimated amounts due to third-party payors are principally related to third-party
payments in excess of outstanding balances which are finalized annually after the
calendar year-end concludes, and are typically received or funded by the end of the
following calendar year.

Third-party advances

Manor records advances from third-party payors as a liability upon receipt.
Patient services accounts receivable
Patient services accounts receivable are stated at the amount Manor expects to collect.

Under Rhode Island General Law Title 40, Chapter 8, Section 6.1(b)(2), Haman Services,
Medical Assistance, Provider Care During Pendency of Application, Uncompensated Care While
Determination is Overdue, upon Manot’s request, the State of Rhode Island (the "State") is
required to make payments to Manor for charges related to a patient that Manor has
determined should be eligible for Medicaid and for whom a completed application for
Medicaid benefits filed with the State has been pending for 90 days or more ("advance
payments"); such advance payments may not be recovered, offset or recouped by the
State upon a subsequent determination by the State that the patient is not eligible for
Medicaid. Consequently, advance payments received by Manor prior to the date the
State has made a determination related to the patient’s eligibility for Medicaid are offset
against the patient services receivables included in the accompanying balance sheet.
Advance payments received by Manor related to Medicaid claims subsequently
approved and paid by the State are required to be repaid to the State through an
ongoing reconciliation process and, consequently, are reported within current liabilities,
third-party advances in the accompanying balance sheet. At December 31, 2019,
advance payments offset against patient services receivables totaled $395,697.

Patient services accounts receivable (continued)

Manor provides for probable uncollectible amounts through a charge to bad debt
expense and a ctedit to the allowance for doubtful accounts based on its assessment of
the current status of individual accounts. Balances that are still outstanding after Manor
has used reasonable collection efforts are written off through a charge to the allowance
for doubtful accounts and a credit to accounts receivable. Manor does not obtain
collateral for amounts due from providing patient services.

At December 31, 2019, approximately 35% of Manor's accounts receivable is due from
the Rhode Island Medicaid program.

At December 31, 2019 and 2018, net accounts receivable was $1,802,247 and
$1,112,424, respectively.

Assets limited as to use

Assets limited as to use are held by trustees, principally in money market accounts,
under trust agreements in connection with a self-insurance arrangement consisting of
funds held by Citizens Bank and The Washington Trust Company in the name of Saint
Elizabeth Community Workers' Compensation Trust.

Property and equipment

Property and equipment are stated at cost, less accumulated depreciation. Depreciation
is provided using the straight-line method over the estimated useful lives of the assets,

ranging from 4 years to 25 years. Depreciation expense totaled $670,612 for the year
ended December 31, 2019.

Manor evaluates long-lived assets held and used by Manor for impairment whenever
events or changes in circumstances indicate that the carrying amount of the asset may
not be recoverable. An impairment loss is recognized if the sum of the expected
undiscounted future cash flows from the use and disposition of the asset is less than its
catrying amount. Generally, the amount of impairment loss is measured as the
difference between the carrying amount of the asset and the estimated fair value of the

asset. Manor did not record an impairment loss during the year ended December 31,
2019.

Estimated workers' compensation self-insurance costs

The liability for workers' compensation self-insurance costs includes estimates of the
ultimate costs for both reported claims and claims incurred but not reported.

It is Manor's policy to fund workers' compensation insurance costs accrued.

Debt issuance costs

Debt issuance costs, net of accumulated amortization, are reported as a direct reduction
of the debt obligation to which such costs relate. Amortization of debt issuance costs is
reported as a component of interest expense and is computed on the straight-line
method, which approximates the effective interest method.

Functional allocation of expenses

The costs of program and supporting services activities have been summarized on a
functional basis in the statement of operations. The statement of functional expenses
presents the natural classification detail of expenses by function. Accordingly, certain
costs have been allocated among the programs and supporting services benefited.

The expenses that are allocated include the following:

Expense Method of Allocation
Payroll Time and effort
Payroll taxes and employee benefits Time and effort
Insurance Time and effort

Income taxes

Manor qualifies as a tax-exempt organization under Section 501(c)(3) of the Internal
Revenue Code. As a not-for-profit entity, Manor is subject to unrelated business
income tax ("UBIT"), if applicable. In accordance with FASB ASC 740, Income Taxes,
Manor applies the "more likely than not" threshold to the recognition and derecognition
of tax positions for its financial statements. Management has evaluated Manor’s tax
positions and has concluded that there were no uncertain tax positions that qualified for
either recognition or disclosure in these financial statements.

Manor files tax returns in the U.S. federal jurisdiction.

Subsequent events

Manor has evaluated subsequent events through October 2, 2020, the date that the
accompanying financial statements were available to be issued. Except as disclosed in
Note 8, there were no material subsequent events that required recognition or disclosure
in these financial statements.

RELATED-PARTY TRANSACTIONS
The Saint Elizabeth Community

Community is organized exclusively for the charitable purpose of supporting, benefiting
and making distributions from its assets to its subsidiaries, which consist of Manor;
Saint Elizabeth Home, East Greenwich ("Home") (which owns and operates a 168-bed
nursing facility located in East Greenwich, Rhode Island); Saint Elizabeth Development
Corporation ("Development") (which is a subsidiary of Home and which owns land in
East Greenwich, Rhode Island where Home operates its nursing facility); Saint
Elizabeth Place, Providence ("Place") (which owns and operates a 149-unit apartment

Living, L.P. d/b/a Saint Elizabeth Court ("Court") (which owns and operates a 69-unit
low and moderate-income assisted living facility located in Providence, Rhode Island);
Cornerstone Adult Services, Inc. (which provides adult day care services in several
Rhode Island locations); Saint Elizabeth Terrace ("Terrace") (which owns a 34-unit
elderly housing apartment complex in Warwick, Rhode Island); and Saint Elizabeth at
Home Corp. (which operates a licensed home health nursing care provider).
Community is also the sole shareholder of SEAL Melrose, Inc. ("SEAL") (which is the
general partner of Court). All subsidiaries, except for SEAL and Court, are organized as
nonprofit organizations.

In a prior year, Community provided Manor approximately $513,000 for operating
needs, the amount is non-interest bearing and has an extended timeframe for
repayment. There are no repayment terms.

Shared services

Home ptovides Manor with administrative and development services; Manor
reimburses Home at an amount equal to Home's cost. The cost, including payroll taxes
and employee benefits, totaled $591,292 for the year ended December 31, 2019, and is

included principally in payroll and contracted setvices in the accompanying statement of
operations.

Self-insurance

Community maintains a pooled self-insurance plan for workers’ compensation costs.
The plan requires the payment of certain administration, claims and risk management
costs, as well as payment for losses. Manor and Home are the principal members of the
plan and make monthly contributions (premiums) to pay for the costs and bond fees, as
well as to establish a cumulative reserve for future claims. The reserve is held in trust
and invested by the plan trustee. Upon termination, any monies in excess of amounts
needed to pay claims and expenses will be returned to the members. The trustee
maintains separate accountability for Manor's allocable portion of contributions, costs,
losses and cumulative reserve for future claims. Manor's cost under the plan totaled
$215,770 for the year ended December 31, 2019.

MEDICAID ELIGIBILITY DETERMINATION DELAYS AND
CONTINGENCY

Medicaid eligibility determination delays

Since early 2016, the State has been significantly delayed in rendering Medicaid eligibility
determinations with respect to patients that Manor has determined should be eligible for
Medicaid and for whom a completed application for Medicaid benefits has been filed
with the State. Manor provides services to these patients and recognizes related revenue
during the period in which the State has not made a determination related to a
completed application filed with the State ("Medicaid Pending Application"). Amounts
reported as patient services receivables related to Medicaid Pending Applications that
have been pending for less than 90 days and those that have been pending for 90 days
or more for which Manor has not requested an advance payment (see Note 1) may be
subject to subsequent denial by the State at some future date. Management believes that
the State will ultimately determine that patients for whom a Medicaid Pending
Application exists are eligible for Medicaid and that any potential loss related to
Medicaid Pending Applications subsequently denied by the State will not have a material
adverse effect on Manot’s financial position.

Continuing operations

‘The accompanying financial statements have been prepared assuming that Manor will
continue as a going concern. Manor has incurred multiple consecutive years of losses,
which have resulted in a net asset deficiency. Operationally, Manor has been challenged
by the impact of COVID-19, and has addressed cash flow needs by increasing private
room tates on January 1, 2020, obtaining provider relief funds (Note 8) and adjusting
personnel staffing to match census swings. The resumption of routine operations is
dependent in part on the successful implementation of these and other strategies.

Community has demonstrated its continued willingness and financial ability to provide
support to Manor as needed to fund Manor's continuing operations and cash flow
needs, and has committed to provide future support through the COVID-19 pandemic
(Note 8) and into 2021. Community is also in a strategic planning implementation
phase, and the role of Manor is constantly being monitored and reviewed.

NOTE 8.

SAINT ELIZABETH MANOR, EAST BAY
NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS

DECEMBER 31, 2019
SUBSEQUENT EVENT

In March 2020, the World Health Organization has declared COVID-19 to constitute a
"Public Health Emergency of International Concern." Disruptions to Manor's business
operations have occurred and could continue to develop as a result from the supply and
quarantine of employees, residents, and vendors. Manor has experienced a decline in
admissions and census and additional costs associated with staffing and protective
medical supplies due to the COVID-19 pandemic. In response, Manor requested and
received a 3-month advance from Medicare totaling approximately $200,000 on future
billings, which it has subsequently repaid. In addition, Manor received approximately
$868,500 from the Provider Relief Fund, to be used to respond to expenses related to
COVID-19 and lost revenue, a Medicaid temporary increase of $115,000, Medicaid
Workforce Stabilization funding of $191,664, and a State of Rhode Island Nursing
Facility Support fund allocation of $106,400. Given the uncertainty of this situation, the
financial impact to Manor, if any, cannot be reasonably estimated at this time.

To Whom It May Concern,

We are submitting this letter in connection with Silver Creek Manor SNF LLC, Berkshire Place SNF,
LLC, and GreenTree Nursing and Rehab LLC’s (collectively, the “Applicant”) applications for a Change
in Effect Control of Silver Creek Manor, Berkshire Place Nursing & Rehabilitation Center, and Saint
Elizabeth Manor East Bay (collectively, the “Applications”), respectively. The financial information
provided to you, compiled by the Applicant and attached to this application at Tab 28, accurately presents
the financial condition and results of operations of Preferred Care at Wall, Preferred Care at Mercer,
Preferred Care at Old Bridge, Preferred Care at Absecon, Preferred Care at Hamilton, Aria Nursing &
Rehab, Allegra Nursing & Rehab, Avista Nursing & Rehab, Carriage House Nursing & Rehab (purchased
November 1, 2020), Collanades Assisted Living (purchased November 1, 2020), Potomac Valley,
Solomons (purchased September 1, 2020), and Hermitage AL (purchased September 1, 2020) through
November 30, 2020. Unless specifically noted, the facilities were owned prior to 2020.

    1) Page 1 of the attachment presents the (i) bed licenses, (ii) revenue, (iii) operating expenses,
       EBITDAR, (iv) Cash on Hand at January 31, 2021, and (v) the Retained Profits in each of the
       Facilities through November 30, 2020.
    2) Page 2 shows the Management Income Net Profit and Net Cash Flow for the management
       companies which are under common control.

In evaluating the Applicant’s affiliated entities’ financial condition, the EBITDAR, and Cash flow, show
the Entities ability to earn income from the Facilities, and the retained profits show the significant
investment made in the facilities.

Additionally, the Applicants’ affiliates have been in the nursing home industry for many years and have
never experienced any failures or defaults. The Applicants’ affiliates also have the banking and industry
relationships needed to ensure success.

Based on the financial information provided herein, the history of the Applicants’ principals and
investors’ successful operation of skilled nursing facilities, the tax returns which we prepare on behalf of
the owners of the facilities, the certified audits we have prepared for other facilities owned and operated
by investors in the Applicants, we certify that in our professional opinion, the Applicants have the
financial ability to consummate the purchase of and to safely operate Silver Creek Manor, Berkshire Place
Nursing & Rehabilitation Center, and Saint Elizabeth Manor East Bay.

                                     LETTER AGREEMENT

       THIS LETTER AGREEMENT (“Agreement”), dated as of January 28, 2021 is among
GREEN TREE NURSING AND REHAB LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company
(“Operator”) and BORUCH MERMELSTEIN, DOV GREEN, SIMON STERN AND AHARON
STERN (“Investors”).

                                        W I T N E S S E T H:

       A.      Operator has submitted its application to the Rhode Island of Health for approval
to assume the operations of the facility located at 1 Dawn Hill Road, Bristol, Rhode Island 02809.
commonly known as St. Elizabeth Manor (the “Facility”).

        B.     As a condition of receiving the consent of the Rhode Island Department of Health
for the Operator to assume said operations, the Rhode Island Department of Health has requested
that the Operator demonstrate financial stability and capital in an amount to operate the Facility,
absent revenue, at full capacity, for no less than 30 days.

       C.      Investors, who are members of the Operator’s entity, personally agree to fund the
required capital to meet the requirements of the Rhode Island Department of Health.

       NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual covenants herein
contained, and for other good and valuable consideration, it is hereby agreed as follows:

      1.      Annexed hereto as Exhibit A are the proof of funds from the Investors that
demonstrate their financial ability to comply with the requirements of the Rhode Island
Department of Health.

        2.      Investors hereby state that they will fund the necessary amounts of cash to the
Facility, for any and all operational needs, in the event the Facility is required to fund, in full, the
day to day operations of the Facility for a minimum of thirty (30) days.

       3.      This Agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the respective
successors and assigns of each of the parties hereto, but does not otherwise create, and shall not be
construed as creating, any rights enforceable by any person not a party to this Agreement.

        4.      This Agreement shall be governed as to validity, interpretations, enforcement and
effect by the laws of the State of Rhode Island without giving effect to conflicts of law principles
thereunder.

        5.      This Agreement may be signed by the parties hereto in several counterparts.
Delivery of a photocopy, facsimile or .pdf copy of an executed counterpart of this Agreement shall
be effective as delivery of a manually executed original counterpart of this Agreement.
